## Track: harness-improvement-loop
<!-- gate: UFH-01#harness-selection-and-integration -->

> Folded from `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md` @ `d221498dc150` on 2026-11-03 (BRF-T7-F6, route 1b, operator D1/D5; retired row UFH-08 → UFH-01). Body moved verbatim, headings demoted two levels to nest under this track; every open and closed box kept its text and ID.

### Harness Improvement Loop — self-improvement for orchestrator-side harness features

> **Fold destination** (BRF-T1-F13, BRF-T1-1, route 1a, 2026-11-02): absorbs 1 handoff(s) as `## Track:` sections at the end of this file (`opencode-shell-integration.md`). The body above the first track is the former `harness-improvement-loop.md`.

**Status**: HIL-1..5 design complete; architecture **B (sibling loop on shared substrate) selected by the operator 2026-10-22**; extraction build started (seam S1 + `src/harness_loop/` skeleton on orchestrator main); runtime loop not started (operator 2026-09-16 deferment retained)
**Created**: 2026-09-16 (operator question during HS-4: "if we want an agent loop for improving the user-facing harness, would it need to be folded into autopilot?")
**Categories**: agent_architecture, autopilot, context_management
**Related (up)**: [`harness-selection-and-integration.md`](harness-program.md#track-harness-selection-and-integration) (HS-4 decision: thin off-the-shelf shell, Hermes-style features built **inside the orchestrator** and exposed as MCP tools / API behaviour)
**Related (substrate)**: [`autopilot-continuous-optimization.md`](autopilot-program.md), [`promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`](autopilot-program.md#track-promptforge-mutation-safety-contract), [`objective-task-rate-goodput.md`](autopilot-program.md#track-objective-task-rate-goodput), [`episodic-memory-integrity.md`](memory-program.md) (M-12 memory instruments)
**Design inputs**: `docs/design/hs4-harness-decision-package-20260916.md`, `docs/design/hs4-shell-and-orchestrator-features-20260916.md` (feature map §3, landed 2026-09-16)

#### Why this exists

HS-4 put the user-facing features (user-profile memory, notes, session search, background review,
delegation, compaction, skills) in the orchestrator so every model call stays on our router and every
feature is measurable. A consequence: those features become **files the improvement machinery can
mutate and measure**. This stub records how a future improvement loop over them should be built, so
the decision is not re-derived.

**The mutation object is bounded.** The **mutable arm** is the feature prompts and parameters the eval
suite measures (summarisation/review/delegation prompts, compaction thresholds, memory-recall knobs).
**Policy documents** — `AGENTS.md`, `SKILL.md` folders, `HARNESS_RUN_POLICY.md` — are **proposal-only
and human-applied**: the loop may draft a diff, never write one, exactly as P5's skill export is a
reviewed commit (HS-4 §3.7 "do not rebuild autonomous skill creation", HS-7 re-targetability, HS-5b
freeze-before-tuning; intake-1323#record, intake-1339#record).

#### Framing (from the 2026-09-16 discussion)

The loop does **not** have to be folded into AutoPilot, but it should **reuse AutoPilot's substrate**
rather than rebuild it. The substrate already provides everything such a loop needs, and each piece
was hard-won:

- mutation proposal (prompt / code / GEPA species) with the MHS-3/4 leakage and risk guards;
- journal + W3 snapshots; AP-55 infra fingerprint / regime digest and comparability verdicts;
- the promotion gate with live-scope frontier, empty-frontier reproduction rule, fail-closed guard,
  file-sha content identity (gate-frontier, 2026-09-16);
- AP-53 re-proposal ledger, AP-54 eval knowledge fence, belief-kernel write-side adapters.

**HIL-4 decision-package draft** (options + tradeoffs; recommendation B). The package is complete
when HIL-3 attaches the substrate-extraction cost to row B and HIL-2 attaches the objective axes to row A.

| Option | Shape | Tradeoff |
|---|---|---|
| A — new AutoPilot species/tier | harness features as another mutation target with their own objective | max reuse; AutoPilot grows a new objective axis + eval suite |
| **B — sibling loop on shared substrate (preferred)** | separate "harness loop" process importing AutoPilot's journal/gate/guards/fingerprints; own proposer, objective, schedule | clean ownership, no churn in live AutoPilot, same safety/evidence rules; needs the substrate refactored into importable components |
| C — fully separate loop | its own journal/gate/guards | fastest start, but re-lives the fail-open / stale-frontier / leakage / comparability bugs already fixed — **not recommended** |

Out of scope for any loop: pure shell surface (OpenCode/pi TUI, permission rules, plugin config) —
config-tunable only, manual.

- Do not cite SWE-chat's 46% pushback, the 79% invisible-failure share, Transluce's ~2% or SWE-Together's -0.92 as EPYC coding-agent base rates (intake-2397#record, intake-2426#record, intake-2427#record, intake-2428#record).
- CODESKILL's +11.03/+5.10 is single-run, transductive and the v2 gain came from re-running only the proposed method; it is not evidence for learned skill management (intake-2392#record).
- LLM-mediated memory consolidation needs a paired accuracy-per-cost readout against a BM25 baseline (intake-2408#record); observation masking stays the default compaction (intake-2409#record).
- Codex exports metrics to Statsig by default unless analytics is disabled (intake-2436#record); flagged to the operator, no setting changed.
- Beacon yields no AutoKernel hypotheses (intake-2387#record).
- The expertise gradient (more planning, fewer asks, higher agent-output acceptance with experience) is directionally consistent across Anthropic's transcript-rated study and Sarkar's Cursor data, with different estimands; the Anthropic study is not evidence on tenure or interrupts; stratify our mined failure modes by session type before reading a gradient into them (intake-2445#record, intake-2448#record).
- GitHub coding-agent adoption estimates from co-authored commit and PR traces (Robbes et al., two studies) are detection lower bounds and context only, never EPYC coding-agent base rates (intake-2459#record, intake-2460#record).

#### HIL-1 accepted home classification

| HS-4 §3 home | Loop-mutable measured arm vs proposal-only/policy boundary |
|---|---|
| §3.1 User-profile memory — `src/user_modeling/` / `ProfileStore`, `/v1` prompt injection, memory tools | Candidate arm may tune only the measured profile-injection prompt or declared retrieval/selection parameters; identify each by file/config digest and compare with `x_memory=off`. Profile contents, user writes, schemas, injection-scan policy, typed `x_*` precedence, and `AGENTS.md`/`SKILL.md`/`HARNESS_RUN_POLICY.md` are never autonomous mutation targets; policy changes can only be proposed for human application. |
| §3.2 Agent notes — bounded notes scope in `ProfileStore` and injection | Candidate arm may tune a measured notes-injection prompt/selection parameter only after the §3.1 arm justifies separation. Notes content and any project/repository policy documents are proposal-only; repository facts remain in versioned `AGENTS.md`, not mutable memory. |
| §3.3 Session search — `src/trace/` search/read surface and MCP `ms.search`/`ms.expand` | Candidate arm may tune a measured deterministic ranking/limit parameter or a measured prompt only if one exists in the suite. Transcript events, source/session identity, inclusion/exclusion fence, tool authorization, and data-retention policy are fixed inputs/policy, not mutation targets. Search returns snippets; no per-search LLM summary is introduced. |
| §3.4 Background review — `src/user_modeling/deriver.py`, idle/background admission | Candidate arm may tune the measured derivation prompt or declared cadence/threshold parameter. Trigger safety (idle-only), admission priority, suppression during a region claim/measured run or with `x_memory=off`, injection scanning, and proposal-only write/approval semantics remain fixed policy. Generated proposals are data, never policy. |
| §3.5 Delegation/sub-agents — `src/proactive_delegation/`, `/api/delegate`, routing and delegation tools | Candidate arm may tune a measured delegation prompt or declared per-role parameter after suite identity is fixed. Router policy, role/model choice, child tool/workspace permissions, routing parity, and escalation authority are protected policy/control surfaces; the loop may propose edits, but cannot write them. |
| §3.6 Compaction — `src/context_compression.py`, graph/session fold and thresholds | Candidate arm may tune the measured summarization prompt or declared fold threshold/ratio parameter. Session identity, fold-cache boundary and byte-identity rule, failure accounting, truthful `usage`, shell autonomy settings, and measurement/truncation policy are invariants, not mutation targets. No code-path change is implied by this prompt/parameter scope. |
| §3.7 Skills — shell-native `SKILL.md` consumer; `src/skill_hub_interop.py` export | No autonomous policy mutation. A skill document is proposal-only and human-applied; B3 export may only produce a reviewable diff/commit after the separately gated M-11a/M-12 SkillBank A/B. Shell `skills.paths` is manual configuration. |
| §3.8 Explicitly not rebuilt | No loop home or mutation arm: Honcho cloud modeling, session re-keying, search-time LLM summaries, autonomous skill creation, iteration-limit summary, multi-platform gateway. Preserve the stated boundaries; do not create new feature tasks from this section. |

      - 2026-10-23 (3c4ca599) RUN-1 re-measured offline from `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/hil15a-flagon-out/run-20261022T192953Z/driver/` (`events.sqlite` + `progress/outcomes.jsonl`; 56 step-bearing runs, 54 pass / 2 fail, the 2 fails both `lcg-hard`). Precision = fires on failed runs / all fires: **`premature_wrap_up` 0/34 (0%; recall 0/2)**, **`zero_step_violation` 2/22 (9.1%; recall 2/2)**. Why they fired (read from the step shapes and the CAS args): (a) all 34 wrap-ups were `file_write_safe` (mutating by name) then `peek` then final, or write then final; the task prompt itself says "write_file, then read_file that same file back, then answer", and `peek` was not in the verifying vocabulary, so the prescribed read-back counted as no verification (30 of 34 had a `peek` after the last write; 4 skipped it). (b) all 22 zero-step firings were runs with no tool call at all (20 passes answered correctly from the inline text, the 2 fails were nudge-exhausted message-only runs), and `tool_required` was never captured (defaults False) while the no-tool branch ignored it. Neither signature is a failure detector on this task family: the answer-regex checker does not test the protocol.
      - 2026-10-23 FIX (orchestrator `13e693a4`, on main; `src/trace/l2/{detectors,model,loaders,report}.py`, `scripts/trace/l2_scan.py`, `tests/unit/test_l2_hil15c_precision.py` + 2 updated tests, 32 pass): (1) `premature_wrap_up`: a successful non-mutating retrieval call (`peek`, `grep`, `list_dir`, `file_info`, `read_file`, `file_read`) after the last mutation counts as read-back verification (`DetectorConfig.readback_verifies`, default True; False restores the old definition); known limit, arguments are not captured with step events so it cannot prove the read touched the mutated path. (2) `zero_step_violation` needs `tool_required is True`; `L2Trace.tool_required` is now tri-state (None = not captured = unobserved, the trace leaves that category's denominator, same convention as task-level logs). `l2_scan.py --tasks <driver tasks json>` sets it per task key (explicit `tool_required`, else the prompt must say "use the <x> tool"). (3) the report gains `category_precision_vs_outcome` (tp/fp/fn/tn, precision, recall per category; None when a category never fired). Re-run on RUN-1: `premature_wrap_up` fires 4/54 passes, 0/2 fails (the 4 are write then final with the prescribed read-back skipped, so correct about the protocol, uncorrelated with the outcome); `zero_step_violation` with `--tasks hil15a_tasks.json` still fires on 24/46 tool-requiring passes (20 no-tool answers plus 4 runs whose first turn was a message) and 2/2 fails = precision 2/26 (7.7%), and WITHOUT `--tasks` it is unobserved (no claim). So the fix removes the false claims, it does not make either category a failure predictor: both stay conformance indicators, not failure signals, until HIL-15b shows otherwise.

- DPF-W4-QUANTUM-CIRCUITS-Q3 (intake-2334): Measure the actual tail frequency with a fast simulator (403M samples) and size the reserve to it rather than to the worst case.
- DPF-W4-REFLECT-F2 (intake-2335): Corpus: WTQ table-QA traces, 119 labelled, two annotators each, pairwise exact 0.78, ordinal alpha 0.82, no extra guidelines; gold is set-valued (a prediction matching either annotator's step is corr…
- DPF-W4-REFLECT-F5 (intake-2335): Reuses TRAIL's GAIA and SWE traces and labels (83 and 30 labelled), so no new corpus there.

- DPF-W4-SPECTRUM-F3 (intake-2336): Results are n=58 and n=126 with one run, no confidence interval, no variance over replays beyond a 5-seed subsample for K; baselines are copied numbers from another paper with a different judge model…
- DPF-W4-SPECTRUM-F4 (intake-2336): Accuracy rises with replay count (action-level mean 46.0, 47.6, 50.8 correct at K=5,10,15) and peaks at moderate log length; short logs (<10 steps) give little spectrum.
- DPF-W4-SPECTRUM-F5 (intake-2336): Hard requirement: a replayable task with outcome variance. Tasks that always fail give no passing spectrum. This is a real constraint, not a missing tool.
- DPF-W4-TRACEELEPHANT-E2 (intake-2339): Label reliability: Krippendorff alpha 0.72 agent, 0.64 step, but the appendix says first-round traces were split evenly among the three annotators, so the overlap behind alpha is not described. Final…

- DPF-W4-TRACEELEPHANT-E4 (intake-2339): Replay-based (dynamic) attribution adds about 10% relative (30.3 to 33.3 points) over static agentic on TraceElephant.
- DPF-W4-TRACEELEPHANT-E5 (intake-2339): Recoverability-aware decisive-step definition (attribute to the missed recovery by a verifier or orchestrator).
- DPF-W4-TRACEELEPHANT-E6 (intake-2339): The '21% of Who&When failures cannot be attributed from output-only logs' claim has no method in the text read.

- DPF-W4-WHENWHOPRO-P5 (intake-2340): Judge protocol evidence: all-at-once beats step-by-step by 7.6 points; step-by-step flags a step before the true one (early commitment); supplying the ground-truth answer helps perception attribution…
- DPF-W4-WHENWHOPRO-P7 (intake-2340): Pro cites AgentRx at 115 (v1).

##### K14 — Harness, vendor-benchmark and cost-measurement evidence

- DPF-1011-1011-04 (intake-1011): Shipped paired per-game data show the log gain is heavy-tailed: mean +20.9pp, median +13.0, +10.8 without the top 4 games, 4 of 25 games negative.
- DPF-1011-1011-05 (intake-1011): The "no-log" control in the shipped harness still allows persistent workspace notes, so it is not a no-memory control.
- DPF-1011-1011-08 (intake-1011): Log-as-untrusted-data rule and self-read exclusion (reads of the log are not recorded) as precedent for any REPL history log.
- DPF-1313-R1 (intake-1313): L0-L3 state-cache vocabulary and the name "agentic garbage collection" for the L2 (REPL values and subagent sessions) mechanism.
- DPF-1313-R3 (intake-1313): Seven-day Factorio run: shallow, wide tree (633 depth-one subagents, 149 waves, at most seven active). Observed maximum, n=1, no ablation.
- DPF-1313-R4 (intake-1313): Refinement preserved an RCON resource-spawn shortcut as a reusable skill despite an anti-cheating heartbeat; remedy: least-privilege action interfaces, independent state validation, auditable rollbac…
- DPF-1313-R5 (intake-1313): Recovery semantics (same identity, recreated non-serializable objects) with no journal of uncertain external side effects.
- DPF-1313-R6 (intake-1313): ARC-AGI-3 headline is a cross-protocol comparison against vendor self-reports because native reruns scored lower.
- DPF-1313-R7 (intake-1313): Harness lift concentrates where the baseline harness is minimal or not co-trained (GLM/Pi-mono mean +0.105 vs +0.011..+0.024); two-source shape with intake-1354.
- DPF-1313-R10 (intake-1313): Asynchronous rlm primitive returning a stable handle; family-scoped message queues.
- DPF-1354-R1 (intake-1354): Harness lift is model-dependent: DSV4F-0731 +20 (22 gained, 2 lost, p=3.6e-5); GLM 3.25 BPW 9/5 (p=0.42).
- DPF-1354-R2 (intake-1354): OMP costs 2.2-2.5x time and tokens per solved task within model; the page prices only successes and then whole attempts.
- DPF-1354-R5 (intake-1354): Quant/engine ladder on GLM-5.2 (FP8-OpenRouter 57, EXL3 3.25 BPW 61, IQ4_NL 46, Q2_K_XL 41) is confounded by engine, hosting and timeouts.
- DPF-1354-R7 (intake-1354): Per-task pass/fail matrix (13 models x 89 tasks) is published inline.
- DPF-1354-R8 (intake-1354): About 70% of the page is PCIe-switch / retimer / ReBAR bring-up notes with no measurement.
- DPF-W1-AA-TB21-A1 (intake-2224): Independent measurement behind the 79% claim: 78.65% (70/89) for DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731 (max), Terminus 2, E2B, 3 repeats.
- DPF-W1-AA-TB21-A2 (intake-2224): Harness effect size on one model across rigs: 49.4% stock (hkinsley rig), 71.9% OMP (same rig), 78.65% Terminus 2 (AA), all single model DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731 at max; rigs differ.
- DPF-W1-AA-TB21-A3 (intake-2224): The same model scores 12.1% on AA Terminal-Bench 4.0 (mini-swe-agent, 66 tasks) versus 78.65% on 2.1 (Terminus 2, 89 tasks).
- DPF-W1-AA-TB21-A5 (intake-2224): AA and DeepSeek 82.7 (own harness, minimal mode) differ by about 4 points on the same benchmark.
- DPF-W1-COMPOSIO-PI-VS-OMP-C1 (intake-2231): Same-model harness ranking on SaaS tool use differs from the hkinsley coding benchmark: OMP loses to minimal Pi by 3/30 here but gains +20/89 over stock there.
- DPF-W1-COMPOSIO-PI-VS-OMP-C2 (intake-2231): OMP uses 33% more tokens and 2.06x median time than Pi for fewer passes; cost per success $0.103 versus $0.028 (estimated; Pi at different effort/provider).
- DPF-W1-COMPOSIO-PI-VS-OMP-C4 (intake-2231): Five-task discordance (four Pi-only, one OMP-only) of 30; McNemar p = 0.375.
- DPF-W1-COMPOSIO-PI-VS-OMP-C6 (intake-2231): OMP hashline edits (short content hashes instead of exact-text match; the OMP author's figure 6.7% to 68.3% on a weak model) and Pi-style minimal four-tool core.
- DPF-W1-METR-M1 (intake-2243): Score at fixed expenditure versus score at plateau versus expenditure at fixed score: which metric is valid depends on whether the curve plateaus and whether the model reaches the target.
- DPF-W1-METR-M2 (intake-2243): The expenditure axis must include all costs, e.g. the cost of running an experiment, not just agent inference.
- DPF-W1-METR-M3 (intake-2243): Cost-of-pass = cost per attempt / P(success); frontier cost-of-pass takes the cheapest qualified producer per problem.
- DPF-W1-METR-M4 (intake-2243): Budget and scoring rule change the agent trajectory; within-run snapshots and across-run budgets are comparable only if the run does not anticipate the budget.
- DPF-W1-METR-M6 (intake-2243): A benchmark pass rate is a sufficient statistic only under equal task value or a shared difficulty ordering.
- DPF-W1-PRIME-RESEARCH-P1 (intake-2247): Frozen verify.py with unseen fixed seeds: record requires the 8-run mean to beat threshold with a margin giving ~1/1000 false-pass; the agent cannot touch the seeds.
- DPF-W1-PRIME-RESEARCH-P2 (intake-2247): Records are best-of->=3 seeds picked at 24h then continued; same-config runs spread 54 steps at 24h (about 8% of the 690-step gap).
- DPF-W1-PRIME-RESEARCH-P4 (intake-2247): A negative result is valid only for the recipe it was tested on; strong runs re-open old negatives when the recipe changes.
- DPF-W1-PRIME-RESEARCH-P5 (intake-2247): Strong runs re-ablate the stack after every merge, drop components that stopped helping, and test pairs that are individually worse but jointly better (one late re-probe worth 31 steps).
- DPF-W1-PRIME-RESEARCH-P6 (intake-2247): Persistent IPython kernel workflow: unique-anchor edit assertion and a probe wrapper that restores CLEAN source in a finally block after contamination.
- DPF-W1-PRIME-RESEARCH-P9 (intake-2247): Hourly LLM monitor found no cheating or sandbox escape in hundreds of reports and was then dropped; contrast intake-1313 Factorio exploit persisted as a skill.
- DPF-W1-PRIME-RESEARCH-P10 (intake-2247): A few runs with a paper-search tool were slightly less creative than runs with internet access fully removed.
- DPF-W2-AKITA-OMP-AK1 (intake-2254): Same model, different harness, different PR defects found: a single reviewer pass is not an audit; use a union of independent reviewers.
- DPF-W2-AKITA-OMP-AK3 (intake-2254): Wave-1 overstatement: this post does not measure OMP tokens.
- DPF-W2-AKITA-OMP-AK4 (intake-2254): Universal read tool (SQLite, archives, notebooks, documents via URI-style selectors) and hash-anchored edits are OMP's concrete advantages for non-source artifacts.
- DPF-W2-COMPOSIO-HARNESS-PR1 (intake-2260): Cost per success is dominated by cache share (Claude Code 1.5% cached vs Codex ~70%, OMP ~57%, fresh input 5x cached): token count alone does not explain cost.
- DPF-W2-COMPOSIO-HARNESS-PR4 (intake-2260): 30 tasks x 1 trial per harness: binomial interval at 20/30 is wide; adjacent ranks are indistinguishable.
- DPF-W2-COMPOSIO-HARNESS-PR5 (intake-2260): Runtime tokens counted differently per harness, so token columns are not comparable across harnesses.
- DPF-W2-COST-OF-PASS-CP3 (intake-2262): Inference-time techniques (majority voting, self-refinement) rarely pay for themselves on the frontier; model-level choice dominates.
- DPF-W2-COST-OF-PASS-CP4 (intake-2262): Counterfactual essentialness (remove a model family, measure frontier loss) is a leave-one-out on the producer set.
- DPF-W2-COST-OF-PASS-CP5 (intake-2262): Per-attempt cost omits verification overheads, wait time, retries and tool charges (authors' limitation).
- DPF-W2-FRONTIER-SPEEDRUN-F1 (intake-2265): Screen cheap with one trial, widen progressively, confirm only at a fixed n; never call an idea worse on a sub-sigma gap; do not stop on partial mid-run loss.
- DPF-W2-FRONTIER-SPEEDRUN-F2 (intake-2265): Pruning round every ~8 ideas: drop each stacked component and keep what still earns its place.
- DPF-W2-FRONTIER-SPEEDRUN-F3 (intake-2265): Same model/effort/harness cells spread 94-304 steps (Fable 5 high: 3030/3026/2726; Kimi K3 kimi-code 2974/3219; Opus 4.8 3018/3236) while Kimi K3 across three harnesses spans 105 steps; one run per c…
- DPF-W2-FRONTIER-SPEEDRUN-F4 (intake-2265): Two rulebooks (serial versus subagent delegation) produce records that are labelled and rerun; instruction-contract changes move results.
- DPF-W2-FRONTIER-SPEEDRUN-F5 (intake-2265): A 'never stop / no improvement found is never valid' contract coincides with measured idle churn (about 372 no-op commands, 153 continue prompts, 131 scheduled wakeups, polling 24-55% of tool calls i…
- DPF-W2-FRONTIER-SPEEDRUN-F6 (intake-2265): Per-run cost differs severalfold by harness at similar steps (Opus 5 $868 vs $56) but the cheap rows are serial-era (no subagents), so contract and harness are confounded.
- DPF-W2-FRONTIER-SPEEDRUN-F7 (intake-2265): run.sh and verify.py are not in the repo; the isolation design (bwrap, netns, logging proxy) is blog-only.
- DPF-W2-INFERENCE-COMPUTE-IC1 (intake-2271): A fixed-budget score is budget-censored; compare arms at matched budgets over a shared range and report the budget.
- DPF-W2-INFERENCE-COMPUTE-IC2 (intake-2271): Terminal-Bench 2.0 still rising at the 10M-token cap; +1.3 +/- 1.0 pp beyond the typical 7.3M-token budget.
- DPF-W2-INFERENCE-COMPUTE-IC3 (intake-2271): Generational gains are reach and reliability, not token efficiency.
- DPF-W2-METR-EXPENDITURE-M1 (intake-2277): Expenditure axis must include experiment compute: 70-90% of NanoGPT trajectory cost was experiments, not tokens.
- DPF-W2-METR-EXPENDITURE-M2 (intake-2277): Raw cumulative-best trajectories overstate progress; GPT-5 and Opus-4.1 'only chase noise' once re-validated 40+ times; screen with few runs, confirm at n=8.
- DPF-W2-METR-EXPENDITURE-M3 (intake-2277): Agents curve-fit the measurement target (stop at first validation step; freeze MLP near the loss target); maintainer rejected such changes as brittle even when the technique is sound.
- DPF-W2-METR-EXPENDITURE-M4 (intake-2277): Hardening recipe: agent edits only the solution file; runner and validator root-owned; runner owns and pauses the timer; model returns logits and the protected validator computes loss.
- DPF-W2-METR-EXPENDITURE-M6 (intake-2277): Maintainer verdict classes for agent changes: mergeable good / mergeable but less general / reject brittle / reject no impact; mergeable share of speedup 50-60% of nominal.
- DPF-W2-METR-EXPENDITURE-M7 (intake-2277): Contamination protocol: ask the model to name records after the start state; exclude the start state if it can.
- DPF-W2-NANOGPT-BENCH-NB1 (intake-2278): Agents recover <10% of human record progress at 512 H100-hours; mostly hyperparameter tuning (corroborates METR 'mostly fiddling with knobs').
- DPF-W2-NANOGPT-BENCH-NB2 (intake-2278): Verifier retimes a candidate ten times at p<0.01 and a mean below the previous best (stated as necessary to stop runtime variance being mistaken for gain).
- DPF-W2-NANOGPT-BENCH-NB3 (intake-2278): Judge category list for comparability-breaking edits (move measured work to untimed setup; precompute/skip work; change evaluated data/masking/cadence; change compile flags).
- DPF-W2-NANOGPT-BENCH-NB4 (intake-2278): GPU cost dominates token cost (second source after METR 70-90%).
- DPF-W2-TBENCH-TB1 (intake-726): Re-encounter: tbench.ai and laude-institute/terminal-bench are already covered by intake-726 and intake-369; the wave-1 'ingest-and-dive' recommendation for the board and repo resolves to a re-encoun…
- DPF-W2-TBENCH-TB2 (intake-726): A public per-row report format that EPYC can copy for HIL report columns: accuracy with 95% CI, pass@k, tokens incl. cached, cost, duration, effort as its own column.
- DPF-W2-TBENCH-TB3 (intake-726): Cached input is 92-99% of input across all 35 rows; the same cache-share lever that drives Composio's cost spread is universal in agent loops.
- DPF-W2-TBENCH-TB4 (intake-726): Data hygiene: 'uncached_input_tokens' means total input in 34 rows and uncached input in 1; recompute cache share as cached / (total - output) and never trust a column name across rows.
- DPF-W2-TBENCH-TB5 (intake-726): Effort/cost non-monotonicity: max effort is not dominant; two ties at less cost.
- DPF-W3-FRONTIER-ENG-F2 (intake-2304): Depth over width at fixed budget (n=1 chain best at n x d = 256; 0.91 at n=16). Matches the AK lane structure of long sequential chains per target and argues against splitting a fixed evaluation budg…
- DPF-W3-FRONTIER-ENG-F3 (intake-2304): Evaluator integrity recipe: read-only evaluator and references, sandboxed candidate directory, score parsed from evaluator output not from candidate-written files, multi-seed evaluation. Same trust s…
- DPF-W3-FRONTIER-ENG-F4 (intake-2304): Rerun instability: one model moved from rank 5.68 to 3.54 on a retest under the same protocol. External evidence that a single-run model ranking on an optimisation benchmark carries large variance.
- DPF-W4-AKITA-BENCH-A3 (intake-2322): In-process forced arms carry metered planner turns/tool calls/cost per variant (51 events, 9 planner text turns, $1.20).
- DPF-W4-AKITA-BENCH-A4 (intake-2322): Harness token volume: Claude Code solo Opus 9.31M cache-read vs opencode ~4.8M for one task.
- DPF-W4-AKITA-BENCH-A5 (intake-2322): The 97 baseline was re-audited to 87; orchestration deltas are against the superseded score.
- DPF-W3-AKITA-MIXING-WF2 (intake-2293): Silent fallback made the "executor" write zero lines while runs showed completion (DeepSeek, wrongly credited in rounds 2 and 2.5). A delegation null is evidence only if the named child actually auth…
- DPF-W3-AKITA-MIXING-WF3 (intake-2293): Unforced delegation rate was 0/7 even with strong prompt language; forced delegation worked mechanically (5-15 dispatches).
- DPF-W3-AKITA-MIXING-WF5 (intake-2293): Delegability follows dependency structure; independent batch work parallelises, cohesive dependent work does not.
- DPF-W3-AKITA-MIXING-WF6 (intake-2293): Same model, different harness moved cost 4-7x and API-hallucination rate (n=1 each).
- DPF-W3-AKITA-MIXING-WF8 (intake-2293): Subscription pricing makes marginal call cost zero; local EPYC inference is the same shape (marginal cost = device-seconds only).
- DPF-W3-AKITA-MIXING-WF9 (intake-2293): Only real saving: same-family tier split (GPT 5.4 xHigh solo $16 to +medium $1-3, -3 points).

##### M19 — Failure-attribution follow-ons waiting on the HIL-13 report or a labelled set

- DPF-W1-ANTHROPIC-HARNESS-LONGRUNNING-L3 (intake-2228): Sprint contract: agree machine-checkable done-criteria before generation, as a file the evaluator later grades against. Relation to our stack: a TaskIR acceptance-criteria field that a reviewer grade… — Trigger: the reviewer control plane designs a per-task criteria contract, or HIL-6 needs a candidate-vs-incumbent grader. First step: read src TaskIR schema for an acceptance field before drafting anything.
- DPF-W1-LANGCHAIN-DEEPAGENTS-L1 (intake-2240): Reasoning allocation by phase (high reasoning for planning and verification, lower in between) under a wall-clock cap, where uniformly maximal reasoning loses to timeouts. Local analogue: thinking-to… — Trigger: per-request-reasoning-budget.md Steps 3-4 (running-server steps) are scheduled, or an AutoPilot numeric-swarm knob for thinking budget is proposed. First step then: add a phase-split arm (plan / act / verify budgets) to the planne…
- DPF-W2-AGENTRACER-A3 (intake-2252): Self-correction feedback from a generic LLM critic (CRITIC with GPT-4.1) degraded GAIA accuracy while step-attributed feedback improved it; single-run figures with no N. — Trigger: when a PromptForge failure_context arm is next designed, include a generic-critique arm against a first-event-annotated arm (A1) so the claim is tested on our traces. Borrow no number.
- DPF-W2-LC-TRACES-D5 (intake-2272): If LangChain exposes trace bodies for this share (public run export, or the UI shows them), the 445 trials with known reward would become a labelled doom-loop and verification-skip corpus. — Trigger: a public run-body endpoint for the share is found, or LangChain publishes an export. Then re-dive as a gold set for loop and exit-without-verify detection. Do not poll.
- DPF-W3-GRAPHTRACER-G2 (intake-2305#record): The withdrawn GraphTracer record prompted an untested local design question, not an attribution result. — Trigger: if an origin-attribution use beyond the symptom-level L2 scan is specified, decide whether tool-call events should record which earlier outputs they consumed, then define an independent replay comparison before claiming benefit. The withdrawn paper supplies no warrant for that benefit.
- DPF-W3-LONG-RUNNING-CLAUDE-N4 (intake-2307): Re-prompt on claimed completion ("is it really done?") as a loop scaffold; the same failure mode (premature completion) the wave-1 L2 scan lists as a category. — Trigger: the L2 scan shows exit-without-verify as a non-trivial share of baseline failures. ##### M27 — Build-cost items converted from declines, each with an estimate and a trigger

- DPF-W1-HONCHO-HN-05: build cost self-host Honcho against the local endpoint: containerised Postgres, Redis and vector store plus overriding the API-model defaults, est. 1-2 days. Trigger: HS-PROF-1 or UTM-V7 shows a profile-memory gap that a deriver pipeline would close; one memory system inside the orchestrator remains the ruling.
- DPF-W1-SKILLEVAL-SE-06: build cost provide a local judge backend and a sandbox for Tier 2 and 3 of SkillEvaluator, est. 1-2 days using the YARN-JUDGE-1 judge path; Tier 1 does not address SkillBank entries. Trigger: M-27 flags need an independent validity check.
- DPF-W1-SKILLGRAPH-SG-04: build cost derive a neighbourhood edge set offline from stored embeddings (Jaccard merge at 0.85), est. 1-2 days. Trigger: M-27 pair census finds merge candidates the cosine rule misses.
- DPF-W1-SKILLGRAPH-SG-05: build cost an RL co-training loop (GRPO) is a weights-lane build, est. several engineer-weeks plus GPU hours; no loop exists. Trigger: the weights lane or the continual-stream trigger opens.
- DPF-W1-G6D-SFORGE-S6: build cost pull the images for the slice from ECR-EB-1 and run SForge against the local /v1 endpoint, est. 0.5 day plus image size. Trigger: ECR-EB-1 yields a slice and an inference budget and window exist.
- DPF-W2-TRAIL-T6: build cost a windowed or hierarchical judge for traces of 262K-8M tokens, est. 3-5 days; Percival is a closed service, so reproduce the method ourselves. Trigger: HIL-17 needs a long-trace judge.
- DPF-W2-AGENTRACER-A6: build cost reproduce the tracer method ourselves: fault-injection data generation (HIL-13 fixtures) plus training an 8B tracer on a labelled set, est. 2-3 weeks including GPU; weights are withheld. Trigger: HIL-12 item 6 produces a labelled failure set of useful size.
- DPF-W2-CME7-SGB: build cost a local-endpoint shim for SkillGenBench, est. 1-2 days, shaped like research `b4c0c6b48` (CME-7 adapter in `conversational-memory-eval-instrument.md`); no local-endpoint path is a build cost, not a reason to decline. Trigger: the instrument is scheduled and no local-endpoint path exists.
- DPF-W2-CME7-AUTOSKILL: build cost a local-endpoint shim for AutoSkill, est. 1-2 days, shaped like research `b4c0c6b48` (CME-7 adapter in `conversational-memory-eval-instrument.md`); no local-endpoint path is a build cost, not a reason to decline. Trigger: the instrument is scheduled and no local-endpoint path exists.
- DPF-W2-CME7-AGENTBREEDER: build cost a local-endpoint shim for AgentBreeder, est. 1-2 days, shaped like research `b4c0c6b48` (CME-7 adapter in `conversational-memory-eval-instrument.md`); no local-endpoint path is a build cost, not a reason to decline. Trigger: the instrument is scheduled and no local-endpoint path exists.
- DPF-W2-CME7-SAFESCAF: build cost a local-endpoint shim for the safety-under-scaffolding repository, est. 1-2 days, shaped like research `b4c0c6b48` (CME-7 adapter in `conversational-memory-eval-instrument.md`); no local-endpoint path is a build cost, not a reason to decline. ## Track: repl-embedding-retrieval
<!-- gate: RTG-08, INF-06, UFH-01#repl-turn-efficiency, UFH-01#qwen-chat-template-evaluation, UFH-01#harness-selection-and-integration -->

> Folded from `handoffs/active/repl-embedding-retrieval.md` @ `d221498dc150` on 2026-11-03 (BRF-T7-F6, route 1b, operator D1/D5; retired row UFH-12 → UFH-01). Body moved verbatim, headings demoted two levels to nest under this track; every open and closed box kept its text and ID.

### REPL Embedding Retrieval — hybrid search that returns pointers

**Status:** active — Phase 0 signed, applied and proven 2026-09-26 (embedder pool 0.95× → 4.96×). Narrowed 2026-09-27: the Phase-2 kill criterion is PRE-REGISTERED (REPL-EMB-2.1 ✅); next is the offline eval (2.2); REPL-EMB-0.3 and 1.4 are frozen. 2026-10-01: `context.search` landed as an opt-in experiment arm (orch `b384cdbb`); defaults unchanged; the 2.1 kill rule governs any default.
**Created:** 2026-09-26
**Scratch**: `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/codex-ni-20261027-ufh12-snapshot-gate/` · worktrees: none · refactor 2026-10-31: `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/refactor-01a16fc3/ufh12-r1-successor-20261031/`
**Owner index:** [user-facing-harness-index.md](user-facing-harness-index.md) (UFH-12).
**Depends on:** UFH-07 (tool-output-compression, TOC-SP-3 + trajectory artifact), INF-78 (OAB-8 scouts),
EVL-37 (repl-turn-efficiency S4 gate, waived for retrieval — D2 below), UFH-01 (HS-TD-3 context selection
as a typed decision — coordinate, do not fork).
**Design canvas:** https://claude.ai/artifact/UnjtMaouUFYs9xz5umnwPd

#### Start here

Build embedding-backed **hybrid** retrieval (lexical + dense, fused with `rrf_fuse` in
`epyc-orchestrator` `src/trace/navigation.py:178`) inside the orchestrator REPL. Four invariants hold in
every phase:

1. **Search returns POINTERS, never summaries** — `(source, line range, score, one-line snippet)`.
2. **Reads still go through `get`/`peek`**, so OAB-12 pull accounting and the print caps keep holding.
3. **No hash-vector fallback.** If no embedder is available, degrade to lexical and **label** the result
   as lexical — never silently substitute a pseudo-embedding.
4. **An index belongs to ONE embedding model.** The scheduler chooses among *instances* of that model,
   never across models.

Phase 0 is done (below). **Narrowed plan (operator, 2026-09-27):** the next work is **REPL-EMB-2.1 → 2.2**, the
kill criterion and then the offline recall eval. The saturation-guard policy (REPL-EMB-1.4, B+D) and the GPU embedder
(REPL-EMB-0.3) are frozen until that eval passes. The orchestrator-vs-baseline comparison moved to its own handoff,
UFH-13 [`thesis-experiment-orchestrator-vs-strongest-model.md`](thesis-experiment-orchestrator-vs-strongest-model.md).

##### Narrowed plan (operator, 2026-09-27)

Adopted after a Fable audit of session workspace-8d: evaluate before building, and pre-register the kill criterion
before any more embedder work.
- **Frozen** (marked in place, boxes stay open): REPL-EMB-0.3, the GPU embedder instance; and REPL-EMB-1.4, the
  saturation-guard **B+D** policy. The landed neighbour cap (`neighbour_cap.max_in_flight: 1`) stays as it is, behind
  the default-off `repl_embedding_pool` flag. The policy arms prepared for it (runbook
  `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/next-window-runbook-20260927.md`, chunks A0/A3) choose that policy. A0 and A3 (sched-sat,
  low-duty) had already run 10:15-11:31Z, before the freeze; A3-raw did not run. Readings: A0 (neighbour cap 0) is
  *partial* on `:8080` and `:8180` (the rest is cross-node); A3 (embedder `OMP_WAIT_POLICY=passive KMP_BLOCKTIME=0`)
  is NULL on all 3 ports, so keep the declared env. Detail: `progress/2026-09/2026-09-27-orch-design.md`. Nothing
  further runs for 1.4 while it is frozen.
- **Cancelled:** the UFH-12 **A1 duty sweep**. It was not a checkbox here, so nothing is left open for it.
- **Operator's eventual target**, once REPL-EMB-2.2 passes the REPL-EMB-2.1 rule: B+D, **plus** GPU embedder
  redundancy (REPL-EMB-0.3).
- **Next:** REPL-EMB-2.2, zero-stack-change and offline, with VB-UFH12-RETR wired first. REPL-EMB-2.1's rule is
  PRE-REGISTERED (operator-approved 2026-09-27, OP-66 closed).

#### Operator decisions (2026-09-26)

- **D1 — placement: AGREED.** Embedder instances sit next to each serving model (dedicated CPU cores; a
  small embedder on each MI210). Selection rule: *idle instance anywhere → else the instance sharing the
  requesting model's hardware → else lexical now, index later.* Leading candidate model:
  **Granite-97M-R2** (8K context, ~107 texts/s single server, best Phase-B recall); its costs are
  measured in the Phase-2 eval, not assumed.
- **D2 — REPL tool gate: WAIVED for retrieval, conditionally.** `repl-turn-efficiency.md:14` ("Do not add
  new REPL tools before S4") does not block this handoff, **provided Phase 2 passes its pre-registered
  kill criterion**. If Phase 2 fails the criterion, the waiver lapses and Phases 3-5 stop.
- **D3 — Phase-0 G1 shortfall: KEEP + cap.** The applied placement is kept although `:8180` landed in the
  pre-registered rollback band (0.86 < 0.90; rollback would restore 0.53). Phase 1 adds the in-flight cap
  (REPL-EMB-1.4) and re-measures G1 against ≥ 0.95.

#### Scheduling and ownership

Embedding work is owned by the **orchestration API**: request lifecycle, R2 (nothing outlives `/chat`),
cancellation, a sha256-keyed chunk cache, and priorities. Reuse the existing `scout_stage.py`
admission (`src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/scout_stage.py`, live `/slots`) and its cancel machinery rather
than building a second scheduler.

#### Tasks

##### Phase 2 — offline retrieval eval, then online shadow (decides the method from data)

- [x] **REPL-EMB-2.1 — pre-register the decision rule and kill criterion BEFORE running**: "cheapest arm
  within X of the best recall@k; cost = CPU-seconds + latency", and a kill criterion against lexical
  (if no dense/hybrid arm beats grep/BM25 by the pre-registered margin, stop at lexical).
  ✅ 2026-09-27 — **PRE-REGISTERED (frozen)**: recall@5, M = 0.10, n ≥ 120, paired-bootstrap lower bound > 0,
  cheapest-within-X = 0.05, exactly as drafted below. Operator-approved in chat on 2026-09-27, session
  https://claude.ai/code/session_01FKXdQsgLuwnFVWQ3npGfrJ (master queue OP-66, closed). Changing k, M, X, n or the CI
  condition later needs a **new registration** (a dated, operator-approved amendment recorded here), and any verdict
  under an amended rule is flagged as such. Nothing has run.
  **Rule (as registered 2026-09-27):**
  - *Metric.* recall@k is the fraction of queries with at least one returned pointer that overlaps a gold span (same
    source, at least one line of overlap). **Primary k = 5.** k ∈ {1, 3, 10} are reported and not decided on.
  - *Query set, frozen before any arm runs:*
    - UFH-07's 45 behavioural questions, plus at least 75 queries from real spill-follow and context-bundle traces,
      for **n ≥ 120**.
    - Gold spans are labelled blind to every arm's output.
    - The corpus, query and label digests go into `FROZEN-AT-LAUNCH.sha256`.
  - *Arms:* grep, BM25 (the lexical baseline L is the better of the two), BGE, Granite-97M-R2, hybrid RRF (`rrf_fuse`
    over lexical plus one dense arm) and ColGREP. Each index uses one embedding model (invariant 4).
  - *Cost:* CPU-seconds per query, with the index build reported separately, and p50/p95 query latency.
  - **Kill rule (applied first).** UFH-12 continues past Phase 2 only if hybrid recall@5 − L ≥ **M = 0.10** AND the
    paired (by-query) bootstrap 95% lower bound of that difference is > 0. Otherwise UFH-12 **stops at lexical**:
    - the D2 waiver lapses;
    - Phases 3-5 stop;
    - the embedder fleet goes to the operator as a keep-for-episodic-memory-or-reclaim question.
  - **Selection rule (only if the kill rule passes):** take the cheapest arm, by CPU-s per query with latency as the
    tie-break, whose recall@5 ≥ best − **X = 0.05**.
  - **Why these numbers.**
    - *k = 5.* Search returns pointers that are read through `get`/`peek` under the print caps. Following more than
      about five pointers costs more in re-fetches than the search saves, so recall beyond 5 is not the working regime.
    - *M = 0.10.* With n ≈ 120 and roughly 30% discordant queries, the paired SE of the difference is ≈ 0.05, so 0.10
      is about 2 SE, the smallest effect this n separates from zero. It is also the size of gain that could pay for
      the embedder fleet's standing cost: the Phase-0 G1 still shows 9-14% frontdoor decode lost with the pool
      saturated (S/Q 0.86-0.91).
    - *X = 0.05 = M/2.* An arm within half the material margin counts as equivalent, so the cheaper one wins. The
      pair reads: "equivalent below X, materially better above M".
    - *Small n kills by design.* At n = 45 the paired SE is ≈ 0.08, so the CI condition will usually fail. The burden
      of proof sits on the new component, not on grep.
    - *Alternatives for the operator.* M = 0.05 with n ≥ 400 (finer, but needs a much larger labelled set), or k = 3
      (stricter on ranking).
  - Report n, per-arm recall@{1,3,5,10}, CPU-s and latency, with every post-hoc amendment flagged.

#### Cache-friendly inlining spec — 2026-10-21

REPL-EMB has no inlining path (search returns pointers; reads go through `get`/`peek`). The shared helper and the integration spec landed on epyc-orchestrator origin/main as `fc02209d` (`docs/design/cache-friendly-injection-repl-emb-spec.md`).

- **[declined: ws58 2026-11-03 content-review: unbounded conditional ("if an inlining mode is ever built"); the spec stays readable here]** **REPL-EMB-CFI — if an inlining mode is ever built, route it through `src/prompt_builders/reusable_blocks.py` per the spec** (chunk text byte-for-byte, no score/rank in the block, neutralisation kept, hashes logged only, OAB-12 pull accounting counts inlined bytes). Gate: the CFI-1 quality A/B in `episodic-memory-integrity.md`. Not startable until an inlining mode exists. (filed 2026-10-21)
- [x] **REPL-EMB-6.1 — Cache-friendly injection layout rule for REPL retrieval results.** When retrieved chunks are inlined (not returned as pointers), fix the layout so a later chunk-reuse path can hit: fixed chunk boundaries (the 256-token embedder slot), small items grouped into blocks of at least 256 tokens (the per-chunk break-even in `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/cacheclip-port-20261021/DESIGN.md` section 2.4), serialisation deterministic and stable (sorted keys, no timestamps or per-call ids inside a block), reusable blocks placed contiguously after the stable prefix and before the volatile tail. Write the rule into this handoff as a layout contract and add a unit test that renders the same chunk set in two orders and asserts each block is byte-identical in both. Cross-reference RTE-LOG-1 so real reuse is measured once the path is live; REPL-EMB retrieval re-retrieves the same chunks across turns and requests (intake-2297#record, intake-2295#record). Acceptance: the contract section exists, the order-invariance test passes, and RTE-LOG-1 records the block kinds this rule produces. Estimate: 1 day. ✅ 2026-10-21
  - 2026-10-21 (dpf-logpfx; orchestrator `5d2140f8`-lineage lane, promoted below): DONE. Layout contract filed below; order-invariance test `tests/unit/test_reusable_blocks.py::TestDocChunkLayoutContract` passes (282 passed across the seven touched test files, region-lock q2); RTE-LOG-1 records the block kinds `doc_chunk`, `skill`, `routing` (`src/backends/prompt_layout.py`). No inlining path exists, so real reuse is not yet measurable.
  - **Layout contract for inlined retrieval results (filed 2026-10-21, lane `dpf-logpfx`; no inlining path exists, this is the contract any future one must meet; helper and integration spec already landed as orchestrator `fc02209d`, `docs/design/cache-friendly-injection-repl-emb-spec.md`).** (1) Chunk boundaries are the embedder slot (256 tokens) and a chunk is never split or re-wrapped. (2) The item text is the chunk's source text byte-for-byte from the indexed range; score, rank, line numbers, timestamps and call ids stay in the pointer record and the log, never in a block. (3) Small chunks are grouped into blocks of at least 256 tokens with `pack_blocks` (content-addressed order, content-defined cuts, maximum 1024 tokens; an oversize chunk is one block). (4) Serialisation is deterministic: the same chunk set in any retrieval order gives byte-identical blocks. (5) Reusable blocks sit contiguously after the stable prefix and before the volatile tail (task text, turn state, per-call fields). (6) Neutralisation of control tokens is applied inside the helper and again on the final string; OAB-12 pull accounting counts inlined bytes as pulled; inlining stays a separate flagged mode behind the CFI-1 quality gate. (7) Logging: RTE-LOG-1 (`src/backends/prompt_layout.py`) records these blocks as kind `doc_chunk` with per-chunk hash, rank and length, and `skill` and `routing` for the memory paths. Order-invariance test: `tests/unit/test_reusable_blocks.py::TestDocChunkLayoutContract`.

## Track: agentic-serving-harness-fixes

### Agentic serving harness fixes — demonstrate on the 27B, generalize, then audit

**Scratch**: `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/codex-ni-ufh14-lb51-residual-r1-20261030/`
Active READY/native evidence; retain for keeper and task owner.

**Status**: active. Phase A: A1 (F12 run) done and graded 2026-10-03; A2 running inside the INF-80 X0 window; A3/A4 implemented and offline-tested, GPU test pending. Phase B: B2, B3, B5 live; B6, B4 (API part) and DIAG deployed 2026-10-03 ~14:50Z, live proofs pending the :8083 restore.
**Created**: 2026-10-03, by operator directive in session ak-ds41-main:

> "yes, i want all the harness bugs we identified to be fixed. The fixes shoudl be as general as possible (not limited solely to the 27B model) as I'm sure they would affect the usage of any of the models in the orchestrator stack. A full review/audit of how fixes should holistically be folded into the orchestrator's mgmt shoudl be performed after they are demonstrated on the 27B model."

**Categories**: agent_architecture, inference_serving, context_management, tool_implementation
**Parent index**: [`user-facing-harness-index.md`](user-facing-harness-index.md), row UFH-14.
**Owners**:
- Phase A and the client-side harness pieces: ak-ds41-main.
- Phase B orchestrator and stack pieces: workspace-ec, the stack owner (formerly workspace-8d).
- Phase C: the operator's choice of auditor (Fable, per the auditor convention).

**Related**:
- [`harness-selection-and-integration.md`](harness-program.md#track-harness-selection-and-integration) (UFH-01, HS-4: harness features live inside the orchestrator).
- [`harness-improvement-loop.md`](harness-program.md#track-harness-improvement-loop) (UFH-08).
- [`deepseek-v41-flash-evaluation.md`](deepseek-v41-flash-evaluation.md) (INF-77, where the defects were found: DS41-C95).

#### Evidence (the defects this handoff fixes)

1. **The 27B harness experiment (DS41-C95)** was graded blind by claude-opus-5-5. P(keep): DeepSeek as-ran 0.75, 27B×codex 0.50, 27B×Hermes 0.33, 27B×opencode 0.25, 27B as-ran 0. Orchestrator arms orv and orsv went 0/4.
   - **Diagnosis:** `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/ds41-c95/diagnosis-27b-report.md`. Most 27B failures were harness and serving defects, not model capability. In 6 of 8 no-reply calls, a keep-class candidate already sat in the transcript.
2. **The prefill/decode share analysis** covers 09-24→10-01 and was parsed from llama-server logs: [`docs/reviews/prefill-share-20261003.md`](../../docs/reviews/prefill-share-20261003.md).
   - On :8083, 74% of prefill time was cold ≥8k prefills where no slot held the prefix.
   - 21k s was duplicate concurrent prefill of the same context.
   - There were 929 prompt-cache evictions and 456 "failed to find a memory slot" warnings.
   - Neighbour prefill dropped decode from 28.6 to 7.8 tok/s, costing about 60k s.
3. **Decode-vs-context probe** (solo, production shape): `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/ds41-c95/probe-decode-vs-context.md`. On MTP, prefill fell from 850 to 487 tok/s between 2k and 80k, and time to first token at 80k is 165 s.

| # | Defect | Layer | Generic? |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Client stream idle timeout (codex ~300 s) shorter than a long silent prefill. The abandoned request keeps prefilling, which doubles the load. | client harness | yes: any model with long prompts |
| D2 | Compaction requests change the prompt prefix (codex sends `tools=[]`), forcing a full cold re-prefill of 79–94k tokens. | client harness | yes |
| D3 | Compaction drops the task prompt (opencode). | client harness | yes |
| D4 | No answer protocol: the model explores past its budget with a candidate in hand, and the final answer is never emitted. | harness prompt and loop | yes |
| D5 | Parallel arms start at the same moment on the same context, so each prefills cold. No prefix warming. | harness scheduling | yes |
| D6 | The `--kv-unified` pool (`-c 196608` shared by 4 slots) overflows under concurrent long contexts, causing evictions and context-exceeded errors. | server config | yes: per-model sizing |
| D7 | Concurrent long prefills stall decode in the other slots. | server admission | yes |
| D8 | Serving telemetry gaps: no wall-clock time, no record of which role or client sent each request, `prompt_eval_ms`=0, cancels untimed, queue wait unlogged. | orchestrator and stack logging | yes |

#### Phase A — demonstrate on the 27B (production Qwen3.8-27B Q8_0, DFlash2, :8083)

- [x] **UFH14-A1.** ✅ 2026-10-03 — F12 run: the cxf1 arm (F1: solo, 1 h idle timeout, compaction at 150k) and the cxf12 arm (F1 plus F2: JSON-first answer, forced answer at 65% of budget, refine turn, 8k thinking cap). 8 solo calls on C2 and C4, graded blind against all C95 arms. Covers D1 and D4, partly D2.

    | arm | answered | keep exact / partial | P(keep) | mean factual errors | median wall s |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | DeepSeek as-ran (calibration) | 3/4 + 1 abstain | 3 / 0 | 0.75 | 0.0 | — |
    | cx (unfixed codex inline) | 2/4 | 0 / 2 | 0.50 | 2.0 | 5401 |

- [ ] **UFH14-A7 (intake P7). Hybrid re-prefill when the next turn's history diverges inside the previous output.** Code read of frozen v10 `ffc1bac82` plus the F12 wire logs. Report and scripts: `/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/p6p7-20261003/`. [check: is A7 a finished code-read finding (close it with [belief: cc] citing /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/p6p7-20261003/ or its committed report) or does a build task remain? Name the task or close]
  - **Mechanism.**
    - Context checkpoints are created at only one call site, in the prompt-processing loop (`tools/server/server-context.cpp:3725`).
    - They are taken at user-message starts and at 4+n_ubatch and 4 tokens before the prompt end (`:3640-3666`, `:3696-3726`).
    - None is taken during or at the end of generation. `--ctx-checkpoints` (default 32) and `--checkpoint-min-step` (default 8192; `common/common.h:633-634`, `common/arg.cpp:1520-1535`) govern prefill only.
    - The intake guess that every turn recomputes the previous output (intake-1827#record notes) is **refuted for the common case**. When the next prompt extends the cached tokens exactly, the live recurrent state is already at the end of the previous output and is used as is. On 179 of 230 follow-on F12 turns, `cache_n` = previous prompt + previous output, with zero re-prefill.
    - The checkpoint matters only when the re-rendered history differs from the generated tokens. The hybrid `pos_min` test (`:3397-3399`, `:3450-3464`; recurrent `seq_rm` rolls back at most `n_rs_seq` tokens, `src/llama-memory-recurrent.cpp:191-200`) then forces a restore, and the newest usable checkpoint is the previous prompt end minus 4.
  - **Measured.**
    - 41 of 230 turns (17.8%) show `cache_n` = previous prompt − 4 exactly, with re-prefill = previous output + 4.
    - Cost: 224,863 tokens and 705 s over 8 calls, 14.8% of all prefill time and 2.5% of wall time.
    - Divergence points come from `selected slot by LCP similarity` in the server-window logs (35 turns). 13 diverge in the first third of the previous output (reasoning), 10 in the middle, and 12 in the last 10% (message or tool-call tail).
    - An end-of-generation checkpoint would save **0** tokens, because every divergence precedes the end.
  - **Next steps, in order.**
    1. *Root cause, no code.* Set `LLAMA_SERVER_SLOTS_DEBUG=1` on the next :8083 relaunch. It prints the old and new tokens at the mismatch (`server-context.cpp:3407-3447`). Then classify each case: template re-render (fix in `epyc-qwen3x-v1-terse.jinja` or the Responses-to-chat conversion) or non-canonical re-tokenization of sampled text. A render-side fix recovers up to the full 705 s per 8 calls.
       - *Infra ready, 2026-10-03 (workspace-ec; a note only, the step stays workspace-89's).* No hand edit at relaunch
         is needed. Orch main aa1d6894 adds `orchestrator_stack.py reload architect_critic --diag-env-override
         LLAMA_SERVER_SLOTS_DEBUG=1 --experiment-id <ID> --override-ttl-s <N>` (N ≤ 14400). The override is recorded
         in `logs/env_overrides/<component>.json` and attested as a declared, time-bound warning (an ERROR after
         expiry). `python -m scripts.server.env_override readback --port 8083 --expect-declared` proves it, and a
         plain `reload` undoes it. Agreed scope: the A3/A4 multi-turn GPU window only, TTL ≤ 4 h (re-apply if longer).
         With debug ON, every `/slots` GET detokenizes the full slot prompts in the server loop, so it is not for heavy
         traffic. Applying it is UFH14-DIAG-EC-a below.
    2. *Only if the cause cannot be removed: experimental-tree code on `llama.cpp-experimental`.* Add generation-time checkpoints every N generated tokens for RS/hybrid slots, in the decode path beside the existing per-round speculative checkpoint (`server-context.cpp:3146-3184`). Each is about 144 MB of 27B linear state (`intake-1847#01`). Restore then lands at the last checkpoint at or before the divergence point. Replayed savings on the 35 located turns: N=1024 saves 92k tokens and 270 s (38% of the class); N=2048 saves 82k and 237 s; an ideal checkpoint exactly at the divergence point saves 105k and 309 s. No flag change in v10 achieves this.

    B6c that was not built) Today a request whose role is parked gets a fast 503 `role_parked`; nothing re-routes
    it. An escalation chain whose next hop is parked (for example `coder_escalation` or `architect_critic` on :8083)
    fails instead of trying the next eligible role. In the routing/escalation layer, treat a parked target like an
    unavailable one: skip to the next role in the chain when one exists, record the skip on the serving record, and
    return `role_parked` only when no alternative remains. Direct role calls and the passthrough keep the 503: a
    named-role caller asked for that role. Done when a test with :8083's roles parked shows an escalation served by
    the next eligible role, with the skip recorded.
  - Declined, not filed: passthrough for multi-endpoint fleets such as frontdoor. Frontdoor returns 422 by design
    (`passthrough.py:236-243`; the KV-pool gate is per URL). No harness experiment targets frontdoor: C95, the
    AutoKernel actor and the opencode/codex local-model configs all address single-endpoint roles. Supporting it
    would mean re-implementing the fleet's endpoint balancing inside the passthrough. The 422 fails loud, so a future
    need surfaces at once. Whether harnesses should ever reach frontdoor is a scope question for UFH14-C1, which
    already covers frontdoor; it is added to C1's scope below rather than filed as a separate task.
  - Noted, not filed: CPU-role passthrough calls cannot run while an AutoKernel window holds all CPU regions. That is
    the region-claim contract working as designed (the orchestrator takes CPU regions per call and waits behind an
    AutoKernel claim), not a defect.

#### Phase C — holistic audit (starts only after A5)

- [ ] **UFH14-C1.** A full review of how the A and B fixes fold into orchestrator management across every model in the stack: CPU and GPU roles, frontdoor, architect, workers and embedders. Covers routing, admission, compaction, caching, telemetry and per-model parameters.

  - Delete-lens included: name the fixes that should NOT become generic.
  - Scope question added 2026-10-03: should harnesses ever reach a multi-endpoint fleet such as frontdoor through
    the passthrough? Today it returns 422 by design. See the declined note under UFH14-B6.
  - 2026-11-02 operator decision: Keep 422 by design (decisions-batch2-4-20261102.json)

#### Notes

- The C95 harness calls :8083 directly through its own proxy, because it needs raw streams with per-request timings on the wire. It runs at concurrency 1, so it cannot exhaust the pool. Once B6 exists, later harness experiments should go through the gate.
- **The 16-token verify block is a copy-workload profile, not an agentic one.** HyperQwen's DFLASH_TOKENS=15 gains come from greedy runs where the model reproduces its own context. Its author puts it about 2:1 behind MTP elsewhere, it costs recurrent-state pages per slot, and the source keeps k=7 for agentic clients (`intake-1827#01`, `intake-1827#06`, `intake-1830#record`). Do not tune temperature-1.0 agentic traffic toward it. Bole prices each extra verify position on Qwen3.8-27B at a ~144 MB recurrent snapshot per sequence (`intake-1847#record`). The production prior for block-8 acceptance at temperature 1 is AngelSpec's ~2.5 tokens per step (`intake-1848#01`).
- Since 2026-09-30 DS41's planner and critic are external models (gpt-6.1-sol, claude-opus-5-5). Whether the 27B returns to the planner seat is an operator decision after A5. It competes for :8083 with the INF-80 X0 window (`/mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/x0-27b-quants/PLAN.md`).
